For the next launches, I need to ensure WANDB_MODE isn't disabled. Looking at the geosafe_s1 logs, I can see the detailed SDF metrics are being captured at step 16200, and the training speed is around 1.6 it/s, which matches the baseline performance, so there's no obvious bottleneck there.

Now I'm compiling the key findings to report: all four experiments are running smoothly with baseline_s1 nearly complete, the WANDB logging issue needs fixing for geosafe_s2 and geosafe_s3, collision rates are improving in geosafe_s1 thanks to the SDF loss, GPU assignments are correct across all processes, and checkpoints including the SDF head are saving properly.

Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

Let me analyze the cleanup log and disk status:

**Disk status now:**
- Root: 30G, 14G used, 17G avail (44%) — healthy
- Data disk: 350G, 267G used, 84G avail (77%)

**Cleanup log analysis:**
The cleanup script has been running on a 10-minute cron cycle, keeping 2 newest checkpoints per experiment directory. It successfully:
- Cleaned baseline_s1 checkpoints: 2000 → 4000 → 6000 → ... → 24000 (all deleted, keeping 26000 + 28000)


- Cleaned baseline_s2 and baseline_s3 checkpoints: 2000 through 22000 (all deleted)
- Cleaned geosafe_s1 checkpoints: 2000 through 10000 (all deleted, keeping the 2 newest)

The most recent cleanup at 08:21 removed the oldest checkpoints from baseline_s1, baseline_s2, and baseline_s3, while geosafe_s1 maintained its 2 most recent checkpoints as expected.

The cleanup script is working correctly across all 4 experiment directories — the glob pattern change to `*/` is confirmed working. Now I'm investigating the "emergency disk cleanup" worker to understand what additional actions it took beyond the regular cleanup, especially since the disk is at 77% usage with 84G free, which doesn't seem critically full. The disk usage has grown significantly from 111G at 04:00 to 267G now, so I need to figure out what else the worker might have done.

Looking at the checkpoint creation and deletion rates, the cleanup is removing checkpoints every 10 minutes but the disk keeps growing because new checkpoints are being created faster than old ones are deleted — the 4 experiments generate roughly 80GB per cycle while cleanup only removes 60-80GB after maintaining the 2-checkpoint buffer. The current checkpoint distribution shows baseline experiments each have 40G with 2 checkpoints, while geosafe_s1 has 59G with 3 checkpoints because it saved one after the last cleanup run.

With ~179G in checkpoints and 267G total disk usage, that leaves about 88G for models and other data. The cleanup seems to be functioning properly, so the "emergency" might have been triggered by a different event or additional cleanup the worker performed. I should verify that all training processes are still running smoothly after the cleanup operations.

The emergency cleanup was likely triggered when disk usage spiked during training, but the cron-based cleanup kept it from becoming critical.

One optimization worth noting: since each checkpoint takes up 20GB (7.5G model plus 13G optimizer state), we could reclaim significant space by removing the optimizer state from final checkpoints that have completed training, since we don't need it for evaluation. When baseline_s1 finishes and saves its final checkpoint at step 30000, the cleanup script will keep that and step 28000 while removing step 26000, which is the expected behavior. I'll write up a brief report on this.

### Assistant

_source line 1495 · 2026-05-20T00:29:22.701Z_

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I see the root cause clearly:

- `train_baseline.sh` line 19: `export WANDB_MODE=disabled` — this is intentional for baselines (no WandB needed)
- `train_geosafe.sh`: No WANDB setting at all — relies on inheriting from the shell environment

The problem: when the worker launched geosafe_s1, the shell session still had `WANDB_MODE=disabled` set from running the baseline scripts. Since `train_geosafe.sh` doesn't explicitly set/unset WANDB_MODE, it inherited the disabled state.

**The fix is simple**: Add `unset WANDB_MODE` or `export WANDB_MODE=online` to `train_geosafe.sh`.


This is the third time I'm flagging this, and it's still not fixed—the geosafe-s2-relay task registered experiments but didn't address the underlying issue. When geosafe_s2 and s3 actually run, they'll face the same WandB problem if launched from the same shell, which means I'm losing independent metric verification and relying entirely on log files for MVP evaluation.

I'm going to escalate this to a warning and push harder for a fix before launch.
```

</details>

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Confirmed: baseline_s1 completed successfully:
- Final checkpoint at step 30000: model.safetensors (7.47 GB) + optimizer.pt (13.5 GB) + metadata.pt
- Also checkpoint at step 29999 (save_interval=2000 didn't trigger at 30000, but the script saves at both 29999 and 30000 - actually wait, save_interval is 2000, so checkpoints at 28000, 30000 should be expected. But we see 29999 and 30000... the final save at step 30000 and a penultimate save at 29999? Or the cleanup script deleted 28000? Let me check...)


The cleanup log shows the last deletion at 08:21 removed baseline_s1/24000 while keeping 26000 and 28000, then at 08:35 two new checkpoints appeared at 29999 and 30000. The next cleanup run at 08:41 would have deleted the older checkpoints, keeping only the two newest ones, which matches what we're seeing now. But that save at step 29999 doesn't align with save_interval=2000, so I need to check the logs to understand why that checkpoint exists.

Looking at the log entries, the script saved at both 29999 and 30000. The save_interval logic probably includes a condition to save at the penultimate step (29999) and again at the final step (30000), either through an explicit check for `step == num_steps - 1` or similar logic that ensures checkpoints are captured at training boundaries.

The real problem is that baseline_s1 actually completed successfully, but the registry incorrectly marked it as failed because WANDB_MODE=disabled prevented W&B from creating a completion sentinel. Without that signal, the monitoring cron couldn't verify the job finished and auto-resolved it as failed after five checks found a dead process. This same issue will cascade to baseline_s2 and baseline_s3 when they finish, and GPU 0 is now sitting idle when it should be running geosafe_s2. I need to post a critical report about this to the Director.
```
